# RELEASE 1.1 FIXES

Defects and gaps to decide or fix before **W1.1-0** ships. Started 11 Sep 2026,
the day after W1.0-0 was tagged `v1.0-0`.

[PRE_RELEASE_FIXES.md](PRE_RELEASE_FIXES.md) is **frozen as the W1.0-0 record**
and takes no new entries. Its two rows that were still open at the tag are
carried forward here as 8 and 9, with their old numbers named, because a row
nobody can add to is a row nobody reads.

[BUGS_FROM_LINUX_PORT.md](BUGS_FROM_LINUX_PORT.md) is the **incoming** list from
SD Core for Linux — what that project found in this tree while porting. It is a
report, not a tracker: it says what Linux did about each defect and does not
record what we did. ***IDS 1 TO 7 HERE ARE ITS OWN NUMBERS, DELIBERATELY***, so
the two files can be read side by side. **That alignment covers 1–7 and nothing
else**: a later Linux finding gets the next free id here and is named as
*"Linux #n"* in its row, because our id space also holds work Linux knows
nothing about.

`SEV` is the recommendation, not a ruling: **B** blocks the release, **S**
should be fixed, **M** minor.

***ITEMS DEFERRED TO W1.2 ARE NOT LISTED AS OPEN HERE.*** Owner, 16 Sep 2026: a
1.2 deferral is held in a `## Deferred to W1.2` section (created when the first
such item arises), out of the open count — not struck (that reads as done) and
not left as an open 1.1 row. **None of the current open rows are 1.2** (owner,
16 Sep 2026).

***THE TABLE BELOW IS THE INDEX. THE SECTIONS UNDER IT ARE DETAIL.*** A struck
number is done; **read the table, never the section headings.** Some entries have
no section at all. This is PRE_RELEASE_FIXES.md's rule and the reason for it is
unchanged — counting `## N.` headings gives an answer that is wrong and looks
authoritative.

***CHECKED HERE, OR ONLY REPORTED?*** Every row says which, and the distinction
is the point of the column rather than a courtesy. **A defect Linux measured on
Linux is a claim about this tree until somebody reads this tree.** Rows 1 and 7
were read in this source on 11 Sep 2026 and say so; the rest carry
***REPORTED, NOT CHECKED HERE***, and the first job on picking one up is to earn
that wording or remove it.

***NEXT FREE ID: 78.*** Take it from here and increment it; **do not derive it by
scanning.** `gplbld/test-fixlist-units.ps1` enforces this line, the uniqueness of
every id, that a section and its row describe the same defect and agree on
status, and that every `RELEASE_1.1 <n>` cited in PROJECT_STATUS.md, HISTORY.md
or a `gplbld` script names an id this table actually has. It needs no install and
no elevation.

| 77 | **M** — ***AN UPGRADE ADDS AND REPLACES VOC RECORDS AND NEVER REMOVES ONE, SO A VERB DELETED FROM `newvoc` STAYS IN EVERY EXISTING ACCOUNT FOR EVER. REPORTED BY THE LINUX PORT 19 Sep 2026 AND CHECKED HERE BEFORE IT WAS BELIEVED, WHICH IS THIS FILE'S RULE.*** **The mechanism is in shared BASIC and reads the same in both trees**: `login`'s `update.voc` opens `@sdsys/newvoc`, `select`s it, and writes each record into the account's VOC. ***IT NEVER WALKS THE ACCOUNT'S VOC***, so nothing can notice a record that is no longer shipped; the one `delete` in the whole routine is the case-rename arm, which removes the OLD-CASED id after writing the new one (RELEASE_1.1 5). **The routine is reached by `UPDATE.ACCOUNTS`, the `$RELEASE` prompt and `upgrade-voc.ps1`'s installer walk** — PRE_RELEASE 70's fix, which made an upgrade reach every account at all. ***THE PAYLOAD IS INERT TODAY AND THE MEASUREMENT IS WHAT SAYS SO, NOT THE ARGUMENT***: `v1.0-0` is `a2e04e4`, 6 Sep 2026, and **exactly one `newvoc` deletion has landed since** — `a47526f`, 13 Sep, which moved `TIER.ADD.ADMINISTRATOR` and `TIER.OMIT.STANDARD` out to `tier.policy`. So a real W1.0-0 → W1.1-0 upgrade leaves an account holding those two, and **64 then deleted `tier.policy` itself**, so nothing reads either: two dead records in a VOC, no behaviour. **The sixteen earlier deletions — `sh`, `config`, `umask`, `sed`, `update.record`, `listpq`, `modify`, `clean.account`, `lock`, `clear.locks`, `list.locks`, `list.readu`, `listu`, `set.date` and the rest — are all PRE-TAG**, so no shipped machine ever had them in an account; they can only be carried by a DEVELOPER tree whose accounts predate them, which is the trap CLAUDE.md already names as *"per-account state is whatever the first install left"*. ***THE REASON TO FILE IT WHILE IT IS HARMLESS IS THAT THE NEXT ONE WILL NOT BE***: `sh` is the shell door, and had it been deleted a week later every upgraded account would have kept it with nothing to report the fact. ***CORRECTED BY THE OWNER, 19 Sep 2026, AND THE FIX IS SMALLER THAN THIS ROW FIRST SAID.*** The row read *"nothing in a record says whether it was ours and withdrawn or theirs"* and called the fix a design. **`[locked]` in field 1 is exactly that marker** — his own 2 Sep 2026 design, `login:1637`, tested on `old.rec` because only the account's copy knows whether a person changed it, `INDEX`ed after the type code and upcased on both sides so `[LOCKED]` cannot fail open. **A sweep honours it the same way the replace path does**, and 10165 already tells the reader which ids were held back and how to release them. ***WHAT IS ACTUALLY MISSING IS ONE SMALL SHIPPED LIST, NOT A MANIFEST OF EVERYTHING***: the walk is driven by `newvoc`, so a withdrawn id is simply never visited, and the cure is a **retired-ids list shipped beside `newvoc`** — the commit that deletes `sh` names `sh` in it, the same discipline `test-retired-wording-units`' `$RETIRED` table already runs for message wording. **That keeps the promise the product prints**: 10166 tells every site *"update.accounts only ever visits records SD ships, so a verb of your own is safe without a marker"*, and a retired list still visits only ids SD shipped — a sweep that walked the account's VOC and deleted whatever `newvoc` lacks would break that sentence and take the site's own verbs with it. ***ONE QUESTION FOR THE OWNER BEFORE IT IS BUILT, AND IT IS SMALL***: a **verb** does not honour `[locked]` on replacement (his ruling, *"every verb (119) because users should not be modifying them"*, reported through 10166). **On REMOVAL the same reasoning points the same way, harder** — a withdrawn `V|IN` record's field 3 is a NUMBER that may now dispatch somewhere else entirely — but overruling a lock on a replace hands the site the new record, while overruling it on a removal hands them nothing, so it is worth one sentence from him rather than an assumption. **Linux reached the same conclusion from source and would not report a verdict from a run nobody could observe** — their tree is stale by their own guard — and their installer refuses to run over an existing install at all, so the route is open there only through `UPDATE.ACCOUNTS ALL`. **Neither port has RUN this**; both read it. | `sdsys/gpl.bp/login` `update.voc` (the `select` at :1514, the write at :1717, the one `delete` at :1719 region); `gplbld/upgrade-voc.ps1`; `sdsys/newvoc`; `a47526f`, `v1.0-0` = `a2e04e4`; PRE_RELEASE 70; RELEASE_1.1 5, 64 |
| 76 | **B** (harness) — ***RELEASE_1.1 64 MADE THE TIER KEYWORDS SYNTAX ERRORS AND LEFT NINETEEN HARNESS SITES SPEAKING THE OLD LANGUAGE. ELEVEN ARE FIXED, THREE ARE DECLARED PENDING, AND THE DRIVER ITSELF IS THE BIG ONE. FOUND 19 Sep 2026 BY READING, NOT BY A RUN — NO SUITE HAS RUN SINCE 64 LANDED AND NONE CAN.*** **`createa`'s keyword cases answer `stop sysmsg(2018, token)` for `ADMINISTRATOR` and `PROGRAMMER`** (there was never a `STANDARD` case — it was only ever the default, so the three keywords were really two), **and 2018 stops the WHOLE command**, so a fixture line naming one makes **no account at all** and the step dies at its first assertion. ***THE ELEVEN WERE ORDINARY VERIFIERS WITH NOTHING TO DO WITH TIERS***, which is why nobody swept them with the tier verifiers: `verify-apiadmin`, `verify-apiidentity`, `verify-apiname`, `verify-apiport`, `verify-apiwire`, `verify-scramlogin`, `verify-vocwrite`, `verify-catgate`, `verify-delacc-xref` (two), `verify-privundetermined`, `verify-uninstallchoices`, `verify-acctmsgs` (four), `verify-doors-admin` (two) and `gplbld/interop-account.ps1` — the keyword dropped, the access keyword left alone. ***`verify-delaccount`'s TWO `ADMINISTRATOR` SITES TOOK `SH-ON OS-ON` INSTEAD, AND THE TEST IMPROVED***: the keyword was only ever the lever that produced an `os.users` record (the thing steps 1 and 3 measure), `grant.os.access` is the same routine either way, and the subject is **no longer a member of Windows Administrators for the seconds it exists** — a side effect the old comment had to declare. ***AND `verify-doors-admin` WAS READING FIELD 5 AS A TIER***: 64 gave that slot to `ACC$SUSPENDED` (`syscom/keys.h:302`) and deleted `ACC$PRIOR.TIER`, so its three tier rows would have read `<blank>` on a healthy account and reported a product regression; they now assert the suspension, and the prior-tier row is deleted with the field. ***THE THREE DECLARED PENDING ARE NOT KEYWORD SWAPS***: `verify-sshadmin`, `verify-apiremote`'s admin leg and `verify-privundetermined`'s composition leg exist to measure an **administrator ACCOUNT**, which 64 abolished — deleting the word would leave a rig measuring something that cannot exist. All three are on 64's own *"verifiers that retire or are rewritten"* list, and all three are still wired as suite steps. ***THE DRIVER IS THE ONE THAT DECIDES WHETHER THE ELEVATED SUITE CAN RUN AT ALL, AND THE TREE ALREADY SAYS SO*** — `verify-apiadmin.ps1:164`, written during 64: *"THE LOGTO SDSYS PREFIX EVERY DRIVER HERE USES IS REFUSED NOW (cproc:2789, 10002) - the whole elevated suite is owed that re-aim"*. Under 64 `CREATE.ACCOUNT` is reachable only from the Windows **SDSYS** account's own elevated session, and `verify-routes.ps1:272-284` is the pattern that survives: **no `LOGTO` prefix, a `WindowsIdentity` gate that refuses any other seat**, which it calls *"the FIRST SDSYS-run rig"*. ***RULED BY THE OWNER, 19 Sep 2026: THE SUITE IS GIVEN SDSYS'S PASSWORD ONCE PER RUN AND SPAWNS ITS OWN SDSYS SESSIONS.*** Asked as a choice between that, a Windows SDSYS sign-in for every elevated run, and dropping the account-creating steps; he took the password. **It keeps him in his own seat and keeps the coverage.** ***AND IT RESTS ON ONE THING NOBODY HAS MEASURED, WHICH IS WHY NO VERIFIER HAS BEEN RE-AIMED YET***: `LOGIN`'s landing case is `upcase(@logname) = 'SDSYS' and kernel(K$ADMINISTRATOR, -1)` — the identity **and an elevated token**, with no `K$INTERACTIVE` term since 64 deleted the administrator peer gate — so **a spawn that yields a UAC-filtered token lands nowhere** and is refused 10002, exactly as the `LOGTO` prefix is, with seventeen rewritten verifiers failing for a reason invisible in their own output. PROJECT_STATUS.md:7334 already records that a local account logging on over the **network** gets a filtered token on this machine (`LocalAccountTokenFilterPolicy` unset). ***`gplbld/probe-sdsysseat.ps1` IS THE ONE RUN THAT SETTLES IT***, and it is the owner's: it measures **both** spawn routes — `Start-Process -Credential` (**expected filtered**, written down so a surprise reads as one) and a scheduled task at `RunLevel Highest` — reporting each one's integrity level and whether `BUILTIN\Administrators` is enabled or deny-only from `whoami /groups` beside an independent .NET check, then drives the installed `sd.exe` with `WHO` from whichever came back elevated. **Anchored on the success wording** (`<n> SDSYS`) with `restricted to privileged users` as a disqualifier, because the account name appears on both paths. The password is typed at the keyboard, never written to a file or a command line (the 14 Aug 2026 rule), and every task is unregistered in a `finally` so no stored credential outlives the run. It parses clean, refuses unelevated with exit 2, creates no SD account and changes no SD state. ***RUN BY THE OWNER, 19 Sep 2026, AND ROUTE A IS ANSWERED: IT IS FILTERED, EXACTLY AS THIS ENTRY PREDICTED IN WRITING.*** `identity=ace\SDSYS`, `isadmin=False`, `BUILTIN\Administrators` **"Group used for deny only"**, `Mandatory Label\Medium Mandatory Level`. **So the spawn works and the seat does not**: `Start-Process -Credential` gets a Medium token, `K$ADMINISTRATOR` is false through it, and `LOGIN`'s landing case cannot match. The row is kept as the **control** — it proves the credential and the work directory are right, so a later failure in route B is route B's. ***ROUTE B IS STILL UNMEASURED, AND BOTH REASONS WERE DEFECTS IN THE PROBE RATHER THAN IN WINDOWS.*** (1) `Register-ScheduledTask -User '.\SDSYS'` → ***"No mapping between account names and security IDs was done"*, HRESULT 0x80070534**: `.\` is a **shell** convention and the Task Scheduler service hands the string to LSA, which has never heard of it — while `Start-Process -Credential` accepts the very same string, so **two APIs disagree about a name and only one says so**. It is `<COMPUTER>\<ACCOUNT>` now. (2) ***THE PROBE MEASURED ROUTE A CORRECTLY AND SHOWED NOBODY***: `Read-Report` and `Show-Token` called `Say`, which writes to the **output stream**, so every printed line was captured into the caller's variable — `$a` came back as a three-element array instead of the hashtable, the token report never reached the screen, and the elevated test would have read `$a.IsAdmin` as **null on a good measurement**. **That is CLAUDE.md's rule 1 arriving through PowerShell's plumbing**, and the fix is a rule rather than a patch: ***a function that returns a value prints nothing, and the caller prints.*** Driven afterwards on the owner's own `routeA.txt` by lifting the two functions out by AST: the lines print and the value is a `Hashtable` with all six facts parsed. A registration failure is now **reported** rather than thrown, because the first run ended mid-sentence with no summary at all. ***SECOND RUN, 19 Sep 2026: BOTH ROUTES MEASURED, NEITHER IS A SEAT, AND THEY FAIL OPPOSITE HALVES OF ONE CONJUNCTION. THE RULING CANNOT BE BUILT AS IT STANDS.*** `kernel.c:299` sets `USR_ADMIN` — the seed for `K$ADMINISTRATOR` — only when **`IsElevated()` AND `connection_type != CN_SOCKET` AND `IsInteractive()`**, and the two predicates read the **same** `getgroups()` list: `IsElevated()` for **544** (`BUILTIN\Administrators`, `sddefs.h:240`), `IsInteractive()` for **4** (`S-1-5-4 NT AUTHORITY\INTERACTIVE`, `sddefs.h:271`). **Route A** — `CreateProcessWithLogonW` — gives an INTERACTIVE logon with a **UAC-filtered** token: `S-1-5-4` present, `Administrators` *deny only*, **Medium**. **Route B** — a scheduled task at `RunLevel Highest` — gives the **full** token with a **BATCH** logon: `Administrators` enabled and *Group owner*, **High**, and **`S-1-5-3` instead of `S-1-5-4`**. So A has the desktop and not the privilege, B has the privilege and not the desktop; `USR_ADMIN` is set in neither, `K$ADMINISTRATOR` is false, and `login:792`'s case-1 guard refuses with **10002** — witnessed, the raw output carrying *"SDSYS Account access is restricted to privileged users / Connection terminated"*. ***THAT IS NOT A DEFECT, IT IS §5.25 WORKING AS RULED***, and the ruling's own sentence names this case: *"it proves local rather than disproving remote — a session whose origin cannot be established stays refused, **which is what keeps an unattended scheduled task out**"*. **The probe now says all of this in its own output** — both SIDs per route, `Test-Seat` printing SD's own conjunction, and, where no route is a seat, a stated **PREDICTION** that SD will refuse, with the rows scored against the prediction so that obeying 5.25 is not reported as a product failure. Driven on both of the owner's real reports by AST lift: 6 of 6. ***WHAT IS LEFT IS A DECISION, NOT A MEASUREMENT***, and three options, each with its cost: **(a)** run the elevated suite from a **Windows SDSYS sign-in** (the option he declined on 19 Sep, now with the measurement behind it — it is the only route that yields INTERACTIVE *and* elevated without touching the product); **(b)** a **third spawn** — `LogonUser` with `LOGON32_LOGON_INTERACTIVE`, then the **linked** elevated token via `GetTokenInformation(TokenLinkedToken)` and `CreateProcessAsUser` — which would carry `S-1-5-4` from the interactive logon *and* the full token, and which this tree already has the machinery to probe (`probe-impersonate.c`, `probe-s4u.c`, 55's work); **UNTESTED, and it is the one worth an hour**; **(c)** relax `IsInteractive()` for this case, which is **changing the access model to suit the test rig** and is the owner's to refuse. ***ROUTE C IS NOW BUILT INTO THE PROBE, AND SEARCHING THE RECORD IS WHAT MADE IT THE ANSWER RATHER THAN A GUESS.*** HISTORY.md:48615, 5 Sep 2026: ***"ELEVATION KEEPS S-1-5-4... INTERACTIVE true in both legs, with `BUILTIN\Administrators` moving FALSE → TRUE between them"*** — read from one ordinary session's own linked token, with the Administrators move as the control that stops the two legs being one token read twice. **So the property route C depends on was measured here a fortnight ago**; what is unknown is only whether it holds for *another account's* logon. ***AND IT IS NOT A TRICK PLAYED ON THE GATE, WHICH IS THE PART THAT DECIDES WHETHER IT IS ALLOWED TO EXIST***: route A already performs a real INTERACTIVE logon as the account — its token carries `S-1-5-4`, measured — and UAC hands that logon a filtered token. `TokenLinkedToken` returns **the other half of that same pair**, which is exactly what UAC grants when a person clicks Yes. The consent is real and it is the owner's: an elevated administrator, at the keyboard, typing the account's password into the probe. **`CreateProcessWithTokenW` rather than `CreateProcessAsUser`**, because the first needs `SeImpersonatePrivilege` (an elevated administrator has it) and the second `SeAssignPrimaryTokenPrivilege` (it does not). Every step reports its **Win32 error** rather than a bare false, including *"this logon may have NO linked token"*, which is the answer if the account is not split-token. **The C# compiles** (lifted from the file and `Add-Type`d on its own, so a compile error cannot kill a run after the password has been typed) and `Spawn` is callable with the shape the probe calls it with; the file parses clean at 13 functions and still refuses unelevated with exit 2. ***THE OWNER'S OWN FOURTH IDEA — A ROTATING DEVELOPMENT-MODE SECRET IN THE CONFIG — IS RECORDED AND NOT BUILT***: it would work, and its costs are that the mechanism **ships** (the check lives in shipped code and `sd.conf` ships), that its whole strength is the ACL on whatever holds the secret, so it widens the gate from *"elevated AND interactive"* to *"elevated"* — **the population §5.25 exists to exclude** — and that any suite run admitted by it is measuring a **relaxed gate**, which contaminates precisely the steps that exist to test the gate (`verify-elevdoor`, `verify-sdsysgate`, `verify-routes`). **If route C fails, that is the design to build**, and the shape that keeps §5.25's consent property is the `sd-elevate.ps1` one CLAUDE.md already blesses: **minting requires an elevated INTERACTIVE session**, so a person at a desktop consents once and the consent is amortised over a short window, with a loud audit record, a banner in the admitted session, and the gate-testing verifiers refusing to run under a ticket at all. ***AND ROUTE C IS CLOSED — RUN 19 Sep 2026, `DuplicateTokenEx failed, Win32 1346` (`ERROR_BAD_IMPERSONATION_LEVEL`), WITH THE MECHANISM THEN MEASURED DIRECTLY AND COSTING NOTHING.*** On an ordinary unelevated token, with no password and no account: **`TokenLinkedToken` hands back impersonation level 1 = `SecurityIdentification`**, and `DuplicateTokenEx` to a PRIMARY token fails with **the identical 1346**. Without **`SeTcbPrivilege`** the linked token can be **READ but not SPAWNED WITH** — which is precisely why HISTORY.md:48615 could read it from an ordinary session a fortnight ago and why this cannot start a process with it. ***READING AND SPAWNING ARE DIFFERENT RIGHTS, AND THE EARLIER MEASUREMENT DID NOT GENERALISE THE WAY THIS ENTRY ASSUMED IT WOULD.*** **What is left, and none of it is free**: grant `SeTcbPrivilege` — *"act as part of the operating system"*, the strongest privilege on the machine — to the calling account, which is **a worse change than any door**; route the spawn through a **LocalSystem helper** (SYSTEM holds both `SeTcbPrivilege` and `SeAssignPrimaryTokenPrivilege`), which works and is **machinery built solely to defeat the product's own gate, with no audit and no banner**; **(a)** the SDSYS sign-in; or **the owner's ticket**, which at least says out loud what it is doing. **The probe now names 1346 and explains it in its own output**, so the closure is not something a later reader has to rediscover. **Written in the conditional because nothing here has been run:** if a route is elevated, the re-aim is mechanical — every account-creating verifier loses its `LOGTO SDSYS` prefix and calls the shared seat instead; **if NEITHER is, the ruling needs the sign-in option or a deliberate machine-wide UAC decision**, and that is the owner's again. ***THE GUARD IS `gplbld/test-acctkeywords-units.py`, FREE TIER, AND IT FOUND EIGHT OF THE NINETEEN ON ITS FIRST RUN*** — the ones assembled across two lines, which the grep that prompted it could not see. It reads the refused set out of `createa` rather than holding a list, refuses the null case, asserts the declared/pending partition (a stale declaration is a FAIL), and its mutants run on a **copy** of the directory with the live files asserted byte-identical. | `gplbld/test-acctkeywords-units.py`; `gplbld/verify-{apiadmin,apiidentity,apiname,apiport,apiwire,scramlogin,vocwrite,catgate,delacc-xref,delaccount,doors-admin,acctmsgs,uninstallchoices,privundetermined,pygate}.ps1`; `gplbld/interop-account.ps1`; `gplbld/verify-routes.ps1:272-284`; `gplbld/verify-apiadmin.ps1:158-175`; `sdsys/gpl.bp/createa` keyword cases; `sdsys/syscom/keys.h:302`; RELEASE_1.1 64, 74 |
| 75 | **S** — ***BUILT IN SOURCE 19 Sep 2026, NOT CYCLED, SO OPEN. SD NOW REQUIRES A COMPLEX PASSWORD EVEN WHERE THE OPERATING SYSTEM DOES NOT.*** ***FOUND BY THE OWNER RUNNING THE CYCLE***: the Linux install demanded 8 characters with mixed case, digits and symbols; ours took anything. ***AND THE FIRST DIAGNOSIS WAS WRONG IN BOTH PORTS, WHICH IS WHY IT IS WORTH THE SENTENCE.*** Neither port had an SD rule at all. What refused him on Linux was **Ubuntu's PAM stack** (`pam_pwquality` + `pam_unix obscure`); what let him through here was the **Windows machine policy**, measured on this box as *"Minimum password length: 0"* with complexity off, a client-SKU default. Both ports were doing the same thing — deferring to the OS — and the difference was Ubuntu's defaults against Windows'. **So "match the other port" would have changed nothing**; the owner was asked again and ruled, on the Linux side, in words that settle the scope: *"The requirement for the complex password is reasonable for access to the database, if possible I would prefer that SD require it even if the OS does not for both SDSYS and any other accounts."* ***IT IS A PARITY DECISION APPROVED IN ONE PORT, SO IT BINDS IN BOTH*** (CLAUDE.md, 15 Sep). ***THE RULE IS A CONTRACT, NOT A LOCAL CHOICE***: ≥8 characters, no maximum; at least one `a-z`, one `A-Z`, one `0-9`, and one **symbol = any other printable ASCII 32-126, space included**; anything outside 32-126 fails outright. **ASCII code ranges on purpose** — it is written in BASIC here and in bash there, and a byte range reads the same in both. **Message 10920** is shared and is shown *before* the prompt as well as on a refusal, because being told the requirement only after failing it is how three attempts get spent guessing. ***THREE IMPLEMENTATIONS HERE, AND THEY CANNOT BE MERGED***: `gpl.bp/pw_complex` (new, `$catalog !pw_complex`) serves MODIFY.PASSWORD, CREATE.ACCOUNT and LOGIN's credential prompt; `finish-install.ps1` sets a **Windows** password with `Set-LocalUser` and never enters SD; `install-sdsys.ps1` draws the **generated** password at `ssPostInstall` before either is reachable. ***THE GENERATOR WAS HARDENED AND THAT IS NOT A TIDY-UP***: its draw could in principle contain no digit, and SD would then have shown the operator a password SD itself would refuse. ***SD'S RULE IS A FLOOR, NEVER A CEILING*** — `Set-LocalUser` still applies the machine policy afterwards and the machine wins where it is stricter. **New status 7 from `!set_passwd`**, distinct from 1 on purpose: Windows refusing a password cannot be fixed by retyping it, SD refusing one is fixed by typing a *different* one, and only a distinct status can say so. ***`10921` IS AGREED*** (Linux, 19 Sep 14:00) — a generic *"That was attempt %1 of %2"*, needed because LOGIN's existing count message is mismatch-specific; they have no `require.credential` and will move their own hard-coded attempt line onto it. ***AND THE CASE BLOCK IS THEIRS RATHER THAN MINE***: my first version tested out-of-range in the **first** arm with a catch-all meaning *"symbol"*, which fails **OPEN** if the arms are ever reordered — an out-of-range byte would satisfy the requirement it exists to fail. **Theirs names 32-126 in the symbol arm and returns `@false` from the catch-all**, so an unmatched character is refused however the arms are ordered. **A shape that cannot fail open beats a check that reports it**, and the guard changed to match: it asserts the catch-all fails closed and *drives* a scrambled order instead of asserting one. **Measured:** all four BASIC files compile under `bbcmp` exit 0 against HEAD controls; `test-pwcomplex-units` **69/69**; free tier **48 of 48**. ***NOT WITNESSED — no session can run a BASIC prompt or the install window***; the witnesses are a weak password refused at each of the four prompts, and a strong one accepted | `sdsys/gpl.bp/pw_complex` (new); `set_acc_password`, `set_passwd`, `login`, `createa`; `sdsys/messages/10920`, `10921`; `gplbld/finish-install.ps1`, `gplbld/install-sdsys.ps1`; `gplbld/test-pwcomplex-units.ps1` |
| 74 | **S** — ***RELEASE_1.1 64 ABOLISHED TIERS AND FOUR PIECES OF SHIPPED TEXT STILL OFFERED THE READER ONE. As much of the complex framework we have built will be removed leaving only the embedded Python and data and password encrypted tunnel. SDSYS unreachable by `LOGTO`; administrator access only from an elevated session **of that one Windows identity**, every other Windows administrator refused; no STANDARD level; every non-SDSYS account, USER **and** GROUP, at today's PROGRAMMER level; ssh **and** the API for every account; OS-level limits are **Windows'** limits on a standard account and nothing of SD's own; keep the embedded Python and the encrypted data/password tunnel; remove the rest. ***WHAT IT DELETES, MEASURED FOOTPRINT***: 183 `ADMINISTRATOR` occurrences across five BASIC programs (`createa` 68, `cproc` 41, `modifya` 37, `login` 31, `apisrvr` 6); The record shape is a Pick `D` item: a header line (`D`, or `D <note>`), the **field number**, a blank, the **description**, then an attribute line and `S`. Measured: `accounts.dic^@id` = field **0** `Account`/`20L`; `^path` = **1** `Pathname`/`30L`; `^descr` = **2** `Description`/`35T`; `^group` = **3** `Windows Group`/`25L`; `^tier` = **5** `Tier`/`13L`; `^prior.tier` = **6** `Was`/`13L`. **FIELD 4 HAS NO FRAGMENT AND THAT IS NOT AN OMISSION TO FIX BLINDLY.** ***AND `accounts.dic^@` IS A SEPARATE, DIFFERENT ITEM***: it reads `PH` on line 1 then `path descr tier BY @id` - a curated list naming only three fields, NOT a complete field map. **So dropping 5 and 6 and adding a suspension field means editing the per-field fragments AND deciding whether that `@` list moves with them**; the `PH` semantics and the attribute codes (`13L`, `20L`, `25L`, `30L`, `35T`) are NOT documented anywhere in this tree, which is why this was left for a session that can read the install's own `write_install_dicts` against it rather than guessed at. ***PROGRESS 18 SEP 2026, THIRD PASS - SLICE 3 IS CUT IN SOURCE: THE REGISTER FIELDS, THE ADMINISTRATOR PREDICATE AND THE TIER MACHINERY. Log `SD-verify/verify-vocwrite-20260915-235830.log`. Linux is not taking (a) for 1.1 (their `PORT_ADOPTION.md`, `access()` is truthful there). *(As diagnosed:)* (a non-administrator account cannot write its OWN VOC over API or ssh, which breaks ordinary use) — ***CONFIRMED 15 Sep 2026 21:44 (`verify-apiwire` run 4, elevated, on the 20:39 install): AN API SESSION RUNNING AS THE ACCOUNT'S OWN USER IS REFUSED A WRITE TO ITS OWN VOC.*** The decisive observation rows: `voc\%0 owner: ace\Don` (the elevated creator, not `sdwire4`), then over the API `WRITE VOC ZZWIRETEST: REFUSED server_error 1 status 3018` and `WRITE ZZWIRED ZZREC: REFUSED ... 3018` (`ER_RDONLY`), while `WRITE ZZWIRE ZZREC: WRITTEN` succeeded to a **DIRECTORY** file with the same ACL. Runs 2/3 (21:20, 21:21) first saw it on a DYNAMIC file (`WRITE ZZWIRE ZZREC: REFUSED ... 3018`, errlog `User 1 (pid 1938, sdwire2): Update to read-only file /cygdrive/c/ProgramData/SD/user_accounts/SDWIRE2/zzwire`), same on 127.0.0.1 and 192.168.0.2 so not the transport. The account directory ACL is `SYSTEM:F, Administrators:F, sdu_<acct>:(OI)(CI)(M)` (`createa:1168-1171`), inheritance intact, so **Windows would allow the write** — the refusal is not the DACL. ***THE MECHANISM, read:*** [dh_open.c:120](sdb_ai/sd64/gplsrc/dh_open.c:120) `if (access(pathname, 2)) read_only = TRUE;` — a POSIX `access(W_OK)` answered by the MSYS2 runtime, and `C:\msys64\etc\fstab` mounts `noacl`, under which the runtime does not read the DACL: it synthesises `rw-r--r--` and grants write to the file's **owner** only (measured in the sibling Git-Bash runtime, also `noacl`: `DON\voc\%0` is `-rw-r--r-- Don`, `test -w` true only for the owner). Every hashed file (`%0`) SD makes in a new account is created by CREATEA under the **administrator's** elevated identity and since 15 Aug 2026 no ownership is transferred (`createa:1892-1897`, *"access comes from the installer's inheritable ACE instead"* — true for the DACL, not for `access()`); the S4U session's euid is the user (`win32s4u.c`, `seteuid`); so `access` says no, the file opens `DHF_RDONLY`, and `op_dio3.c:342` refuses. **A DIRECTORY-file write never calls `access()`** — a real `CreateFile` against the real DACL — which is why the DIRECTORY write and `verify-apiidentity`'s `ZZIDOWN` both succeeded on the identical ACL. ***REACH:*** every non-administrator account, over API **and ssh** (same runtime, same owner), is refused every write to a hashed file it did not itself create — its VOC (so `CREATE.FILE`, `SET.FILE`, `CNAME`, `LOGIN`'s `update.voc`) and any DATA file CREATEA/the installer made. The suite never saw it because every test session so far was the administrator, who owns what they touch. ***LINUX DOES NOT SHARE IT, AND THE ANSWER NAMES THE ROOT CAUSE (mailbox 22:30, SDCore4Linux `8a45a12`):*** their `dh_open` line is ours verbatim, but their `createa` **chowns the new account's `voc`, `voc/%0` and `voc/%1` to the account's own user** (`createa:391-393` `uid=acc.uname gid="sdu_":acc.uname`, applied at `:724-729`), so `%0` is owned by the user and `access(W_OK)` succeeds. ***THAT chown IS EXACTLY THE STEP THE WINDOWS PORT REMOVED*** — `createa:1892` *"set.owner and its seven callers are gone … they chowned to sdsys:sdusers or root:sdusers, three accounts that do not exist on Windows"* — so this is a Windows-only regression introduced by dropping the ownership transfer, on the belief the inheritable ACE replaced it (true for the DACL, not for `noacl` `access()`). Linux witnesses a plain PROGRAMMER account writing its own VOC (`open "voc"; write r to f,"$release"`) green on `c1ea29b`, though only in a LOCAL session — they too have no API VOC-write row, and call the API case expect-not-measured (transport-independent code, session carries the user's uid/groups). ***Candidate fixes, none chosen (owner's call):*** **(b) restore the ownership transfer to the account user** — `icacls /setowner` (elevated) on the account's `%0`s at CREATE.ACCOUNT/`CREATE.FILE`, the Windows form of what Linux does and the direct undo of the 15 Aug removal; it is the parity fix and does not need a C change, but does not help files an admin adds to the account later; **(a)** replace `access(W_OK)` in `dh_open.c` with an open-for-update attempt (read-only only on failure) — runtime-independent, fixes ssh too, and validated in principle here because the DIRECTORY-file write already proved a real open hits the real DACL where `access()` lies; **but Linux flags a caveat worth a code comment**: `access()` asks with the REAL uid/gid and `open()` with the EFFECTIVE, and on Windows the S4U session's real uid stays the service's (`win32s4u.c`, `seteuid` moves only the effective), so the two are not interchangeable and the change must be justified by the `noacl` lie, not treated as a null refactor; **(c)** a Windows `AccessCheck` helper in `dh_open`. ***FIX (a) CHOSEN (owner, 15 Sep 2026) AND BUILT IN SOURCE, syntax-clean, NOT CYCLED:*** [dh_open.c](sdb_ai/sd64/gplsrc/dh_open.c) — both subfile opens (`%0` at the primary, `%1` at the overflow) now try `dio_open(DIO_UPDATE)` and drop to `DIO_READ`+`read_only` only if that fails, replacing the two `access(pathname, 2)` calls; `process.os_error` is saved and restored across a fallback so an update-then-read does not leave a spurious error; the overflow honours an already-set `read_only` (opening `DIO_READ` directly), matching the old form. `gcc -fsyntax-only` with the `sd:` flags: 0 warnings. Linux's real/effective caveat is a code comment. **Witness WRITTEN, NOT RUN — `gplbld/verify-vocwrite.ps1`** (elevated, standalone, `-Prefix <fresh>`, on `$neverShipped`; parse 0 errors / 10 functions, no BOM/CR, refuses unelevated): a non-admin PROGRAMMER account, over a real API session as itself, writes its OWN VOC and a DATA file the admin created, both scored `WRITTEN` with a `status 3018` disqualifier, read back on the server, and a null-case control that both records were absent first; the `%0` owners are printed to show the write succeeds despite admin ownership. **Owed: a cycle** (C change; `assert-current` is STALE until then and the witness refuses on it), then the elevated witness run. *Would then be falsified by:* a `3018` on either write after the cycle. *Falsified-if (the bug):* a fresh non-admin account writes its VOC over ssh or API without 3018; or its VOC `%0` comes back owned by the account user. | [dh_open.c:120](sdb_ai/sd64/gplsrc/dh_open.c:120); [op_dio3.c:342](sdb_ai/sd64/gplsrc/op_dio3.c:342) `ER_RDONLY`; `k_error.c:890`; `createa:1168`, `:1892`; `win32s4u.c`; `C:\msys64\etc\fstab` (`noacl`); `gplbld/verify-apiwire.ps1` steps 3/7 |

APISRVR requires `p=tls-exporter,,` and a matching `c=` over TLS (SDEXT key **110** `SD_TLS_CBIND` - take the same number); the client does not verify the self-signed certificate, the bound login is what stops a man in the middle. **What the port needs that Linux did not:** `sdwind.c` forks `sd -n` with the socket on fd 0/1 under MSYS2, so the relay should carry over, but its privilege drop to `nobody` has no Windows equivalent; the NATIVE UCRT64 client DLL (`gplsrc/sdclilib`, imports only KERNEL32/WS2_32/bcrypt) needs Schannel (whose TLS 1.3 exporter is `SECPKG_ATTR_KEYING_MATERIAL`) or a vendored OpenSSL; and the installer needs an OpenSSL runtime for sd. ***HOW LINUX'S `libssl-dev` STEP MAPS HERE (owner asked, 15 Sep 2026): NOT AS AN INSTALL-TIME STEP.*** `sd.iss:10-13` packages a tree `stage.py` already built and bootstrapped, needing no compiler on the target, so the dependency splits in two. (a) **The build machine:** add MSYS2's OpenSSL development package(s) to the `pacman -S --needed` list. ## 1. `tier.build.rec` still applies the type-letter transformation PRE_RELEASE 136 removed from `CREATEA`

***CONFIRMED IN THIS SOURCE 11 Sep 2026, BY READING IT. Nothing was run*** — no
account has been created, upgraded or downgraded to watch this happen, and that
witness is what closing this entry needs.

**What was read.** `sdsys/gpl.bp/CREATEA:1308` carries the transformation as a
**comment**, struck by PRE_RELEASE 136 on 2 Sep 2026 with a long banner
explaining that field 1 of a VOC record is the description whose first character
is the type code, and that reducing it left every created account with a bare
`F` where SDSYS shows a sentence. `sdsys/gpl.bp/MODIFYA:1407` and `:1411` carry
the same two lines **live**.

***THE BANNER IS THE PART THAT MAKES THIS MORE THAN A MISSED EDIT.***
`MODIFYA:1389` states *"THE TRANSFORMATION IS COPIED FROM CREATEA VERBATIM and
must stay that way: it is what makes a rebuilt record byte-comparable with one
CREATE.ACCOUNT wrote, which is the whole basis of the 'delete only what we would
have written' rule in voc.delta."* **The invariant it names is true and the code
under it no longer satisfies it** — so a reader who checks the comment is told
the two agree.

**The half Linux reported.** `tier.del.one:1434` reads the account's record and
deletes only if it equals the rebuilt one. The rebuilt one is stripped and the
account's is whole, so they never match, and `voc.removed` stays 0 while
`voc.kept` absorbs the difference. `modify.account x standard` then reports a
tier change through 10109 while the PROGRAMMER verbs are still in the VOC.

***THE HALF NOT REPORTED.*** `tier.add.one:1423` calls the same subroutine and
**writes the result**. An upgrade therefore puts bare-letter records into the
account's VOC — the exact defect the owner saw in a `listf` on 2 Sep 2026 and
which 136 fixed in `CREATEA` only. **`modify.account` was not in 136's scope**,
so this is one defect surviving in the verb its own fix did not visit.

**Why nothing failed.** Both halves need a tier change on an account created
**after** 2 Sep. `verify-tiers` builds accounts and counts verbs; whether it
would catch either half is not known and is worth establishing before the fix,
so the fix has a witness that is not itself new.

## 7. `GETLOCKS` dereferences NULL when a lock outlives its owner

***CONFIRMED IN THIS SOURCE 11 Sep 2026, BY READING IT. Nothing was run here***
— the fault was measured on Linux, not on Windows.

`gplsrc/sysseg.h:224` defines `UserPtr(n)` as
`((*(UMap(n))!=0)?UPtr(*(UMap(n))):NULL)`. **The NULL branch is the whole point
of the macro**: an unmapped user has no table entry. `gplsrc/op_lock.c:292`
(file locks) and `:312` (record locks) both pass its result straight into
`ts_printf` as `UserPtr(abs(lock_owner))->username`.

***WHAT IS NOT KNOWN HERE IS THE CONSEQUENCE, NOT THE DEFECT.*** On Linux the
fault landed inside the `FILE_TABLE_LOCK` + `REC_LOCK_SEM` section and left both
held, which stopped every session. This port's semaphores are POSIX rather than
System V (§5.1), so **whether a fault there strands the same two locks has not
been established** and should not be asserted from Linux's result. The
dereference is identical either way.

**Reaching it** needs a lock whose owner's session is gone — which
PROJECT_STATUS.md §"Not verified" already lists as an exercise gap: *"a session
dies without logging out; its user-table slot survives"*. That note describes
the state this entry needs, so the two are worth reading together.

**CLOSED 17 Sep 2026 — the state cannot be produced on this tree.** A dead
session's slot survives only until the daemon's sweep (37, fixed the same day),
and the sweep's `remove_user()` gives the slot's locks away with it (PRE_RELEASE
24), under the same `FILE_TABLE_LOCK` + `REC_LOCK_SEM` that `op_getlocks()` reads
under. So no reader sees a lock whose owner is unmapped. The fix in `op_lock.c`
stays as defence; `verify-deadlock` reports `(gone)` if it is ever seen, and
its 17 Sep run saw `named` then `none`, as this predicts.

## 17. `gplbld/basicfuncs.sb` and `gplbld/verify-basicfuncs.ps1`: the coverage claim was prose

**DONE AND WITNESSED 12 Sep 2026**, unelevated, on the 23:41:16 install:
`verify-basicfuncs` **184 of 184, 0 FAIL, exit 0**, compiled at `0 error(s)`
with no warnings, coverage **176 known = 116 exercised + 60 declared**, V1–V5
all PASS. Linux #8.

***THE DEFECT IS THE SENTENCE, NOT THE FIVE NAMES.*** The file ended its
exclusion list with *"Everything else in BCOMP's intrinsics table is exercised
below."* A comment cannot be wrong out loud, so an intrinsic added to BCOMP
later joined the gap in silence and nothing in the tree could notice.

**What the claim was hiding, measured here before anything was changed.** Linux
reported three names; this tree had **seven distinct faults**:

| | |
|---|---|
| `ASSIGNED`, `UNASSIGNED`, `SWAP` | named nowhere — neither exercised nor declared. **Linux's three** |
| `DELETE` | declared untested **and** tested by `DELETE.fn`. The double count |
| `CHANGE` | excluded under *"change the process"* beside `CHGPHANT` and `CONFIG`, when `gplsrc/op_chnge.c:35` is *"op_change() - Change substrings"* — a pure substring replace touching no session state |
| ***`NOT`*** | ***also named nowhere, and the old header is what hid it***: a scan for known names found the word NOT inside the heading *"WHAT IS DELIBERATELY NOT TESTED HERE"* and counted the intrinsic as declared. **Not in Linux's report** |
| ***`INMAT.reuse`*** | ***a case labelled for one function while calling another.*** The code has always been `reuse(1)`; the label credited `INMAT`, which is declared untested, and left `REUSE` looking untested. **One label, two wrong entries. Not in Linux's report** |
| ***`ADDS.via.SUM`*** | ***named a function this tree does not have at all*** — no `ADDS` in BCOMP's table and none in `OPCODES.H`. **Not in Linux's report** |

**The fix, in three parts.** The exclusions are now declared on `* NOT.TESTED:`
lines. `Get-CoverageVerdict` in `verify-basicfuncs.ps1` reads BCOMP's own table,
the declarations and every case label, and the verifier fails on **V2** named
nowhere, **V3** claimed both ways, **V4** declared but unknown to BCOMP, **V5** a
label matching no intrinsic and not an `OP.` operator case. ***V1 REFUSES THE
NULL CASE***: a table that did not parse leaves every other row passing
vacuously, so fewer than 100 names is itself a failure.

***BCOMP HAS TWO TABLES AND ONLY ONE IS IN SCOPE.*** `intrinsics` is the
ordinary set; `int.intrinsics` (`BCOMP:619`) is the 36 internal ones an
`$internal` program may call. An unanchored pattern matches both and answers
**212** instead of 176 — measured, and it was the first answer this work
produced. Same class as the `nm --defined-only` artefact in §8's constraint 5.

***THE ARGUMENT ORDER OF `CHANGE` IS NOW PINNED, BECAUSE IT IS THE REVERSE OF
WHAT THE NAMES SUGGEST.*** `BCOMP:4064` — *"3, 4 or 5 arguments. Arg 4 defaults
to -1, arg 5 to 1"* — and `op_chnge.c`'s stack comment puts *"First occurrence
to change"* on **top**, so **arg 4 is how many to change and arg 5 is which one
to start at**. Driven: `change('a-a-a','a','Z',2)` → `Z-Z-a` and
`change('a-a-a','a','Z',1,2)` → `a-Z-a`. `SWAP` shares the opcode
(`BCOMP:592`) and is driven too rather than assumed equivalent.

***AND TWO EXPECTATIONS WERE WRONG BEFORE THEY WERE RIGHT, WHICH IS THE FILE
WORKING AS DESIGNED.*** `ASSIGNED`/`UNASSIGNED` need a variable with no value.
A plain local raises `BCOMP:1509`'s *"%1 is not assigned a value"* at compile
time; a named **common** does not, but is not unassigned either —
`op_array.c:140` creates the block with `initialise_zero` and `a_alloc`'s own
comment reads *"TRUE: initialise to zero, FALSE leave as UNASSIGNED"*, so every
element starts as INTEGER 0. **That arrived as two FAIL lines, not as a
theory.** What works is a variable the compiler sees assigned and the run never
reaches — `op_array.c:693` allocates locals with FALSE. The common is kept, with
the answer it actually gives, as `ASSIGNED.common`.

**The guard**: `gplbld/test-basicfuncscov-units.ps1`, free tier, 18 rows. It
lifts `Get-CoverageVerdict` out by AST so it cannot drift from the verifier,
drives all five V rows on fixtures, carries a **live control** against the real
BCOMP and probe, and two **mutants** — a dropped declaration must turn V2 red
naming `DIR`, a double claim must turn V3 red naming `ABS` — both done as
in-memory string mutations, with a row asserting nothing on disk changed.

***IT CAUGHT TWO THINGS BEFORE IT WENT GREEN.*** A line wrap put the declaration
keyword at the start of a continuation line in the new header, and **V4 read
thirteen English words as intrinsic names** — the check earning itself before it
had ever run in anger. Then the live row reported `exercised = True`: these
hashtables are keyed by BASIC function names, **BCOMP has an intrinsic called
`COUNT`**, and PowerShell resolves `$h.Count` to the value of that key rather
than the tally. Every fixture row passed while it was broken, because no fixture
had a `COUNT` in it. `.psbase.Count` is the fix and there is a row pinning the
shape.
